@@ -984,3 +984,86 @@ export function collectLayer(
 			: [],
 	)
 }
+
+/**
+ * Scans one layer's rules for a treatment that depends on where its tag sits in the markup.
+ *
+ * @param rules - The layer's style rules, such as the `CSSStyleRule` values a
+ *   {@link collectLayer} reading holds.
+ * @param tags - The tags to mount, such as the distinct values the `ELEMENT_TAGS` table in
+ *   `tests/setupCases.ts` names.
+ * @param mandated - The ancestor and descendant pairs the HTML content model mandates, such as the
+ *   `MANDATED_TAG_PAIRS` table in `tests/setupCases.ts`. A nesting reported for one of them is not
+ *   a finding, because the markup has nowhere else to write that child.
+ * @returns Each positional pair found, `<outer> > <inner>` for a nesting and `<outer> + <inner>`
+ *   for a sibling, in mounted order. An empty list is the claim that every tag reads the same
+ *   treatment wherever the markup puts it.
+ * @throws An `Error` when the selector engine refuses one of the rules' selectors, so a selector
+ *   nothing could be compared against is never counted as a tag that passed.
+ *
+ * @remarks
+ * The judgment is the browser's. Each tag is mounted alone, then inside each other tag, then after
+ * each other tag, and the rules whose selectors `Element.matches` reports on it are compared across
+ * the three. A set that differs is a rule reaching the tag through its position rather than through
+ * its name. Nothing here parses a selector, so a `:has()` argument, an `:is()` list, a namespace
+ * separator, and every other construct a hand-written grammar would have to model are answered by
+ * the engine that ships them.
+ *
+ * The reading is a set comparison rather than a rule count, because two rules swapping places leave
+ * the count unchanged. It is taken against the tag mounted alone, so a rule that reaches every
+ * position equally is not a finding.
+ *
+ * The nodes are mounted through {@link scene}, so the caller's `afterEach` hook takes them off the
+ * page with everything else its case mounted.
+ *
+ * The refusal is unproven and cannot be driven from a `CSSStyleRule`: a stylesheet holds only the
+ * selectors the engine parsed, and assigning an unparseable one to `selectorText` is ignored. On
+ * Chromium 141 every selector a sheet holds is a selector `matches` reads, so the guard exists for
+ * an engine that parses a form it will not match — `:has()` was such a form before it shipped. A
+ * case drives it on the day a supported engine refuses one, and until then the guard is what keeps
+ * an unread selector from passing silently as a tag with nothing against it.
+ */
+export function scanPositional(
+	rules: readonly CSSStyleRule[],
+	tags: readonly string[],
+	mandated: ReadonlyArray<readonly [string, string]>,
+): readonly string[] {
+	const host = scene.mount('')
+	const selectors = rules.map((rule) => rule.selectorText)
+	for (const selector of selectors) {
+		try {
+			host.matches(selector)
+		} catch {
+			throw new Error(`The selector engine reads no ${selector}`)
+		}
+	}
+	const alone = new Map<string, string>()
+	for (const tag of tags) {
+		const element = document.createElement(tag)
+		host.replaceChildren(element)
+		alone.set(tag, selectors.filter((selector) => element.matches(selector)).join('\n'))
+	}
+	const findings: string[] = []
+	for (const outer of tags) {
+		for (const inner of tags) {
+			if (outer === inner) continue
+			const resting = requireValue(alone.get(inner), `No resting reading for ${inner}`)
+			const parent = document.createElement(outer)
+			const nested = document.createElement(inner)
+			parent.append(nested)
+			host.replaceChildren(parent)
+			if (
+				selectors.filter((selector) => nested.matches(selector)).join('\n') !== resting &&
+				!mandated.some(([ancestor, descendant]) => ancestor === outer && descendant === inner)
+			)
+				findings.push(`${outer} > ${inner}`)
+			const leading = document.createElement(outer)
+			const following = document.createElement(inner)
+			host.replaceChildren(leading, following)
+			if (selectors.filter((selector) => following.matches(selector)).join('\n') !== resting)
+				findings.push(`${outer} + ${inner}`)
+		}
+	}
+	host.replaceChildren()
+	return findings
+}

+import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'
+import { TOKEN_NAMES } from '@src/core'
+import { parse } from 'postcss'
+import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
+import { readBootstrapCascade } from './setupConformance.js'
+import * as setup from './setupCalibration.js'
+import {
+	BOOTSTRAP_DARK_VARIABLES,
+	BOOTSTRAP_ROOT_VARIABLES,
+	CALIBRATED_TIERS,
+	CUSTOMIZATION_RECIPE,
+	FILL_ONLY_RECIPE,
+	RETAINED_COLOR_ALIASES,
+	RETAINED_LENGTH_ALIASES,
+	THEME_DARK_ADDITIONS,
+	VENEER_GUIDE_PATH,
+} from './setupCalibration.js'
+import { collectTokenNames, extractBootstrapVariables } from './setupStyles.js'
+
+describe('calibration setup', () => {
+	it('exports the scope patterns, the retained value lists, and the settled readings, and nothing that walks a cascade', () => {
+		expect(Object.keys(setup).sort()).toEqual(
+			[
+				'BOOTSTRAP_DARK_VARIABLES',
+				'BOOTSTRAP_ROOT_VARIABLES',
+				'BOOTSTRAP_SCOPE_PATTERNS',
+				'CALIBRATED_TIERS',
+				'CUSTOMIZATION_RECIPE',
+				'FILL_ONLY_RECIPE',
+				'RETAINED_COLOR_ALIASES',
+				'RETAINED_LENGTH_ALIASES',
+				'THEME_DARK_ADDITIONS',
+				'VENEER_GUIDE_PATH',
+			].sort(),
+		)
+	})
+	it('names in every calibration row a role tier the token registry declares, in a mode the theme scopes', () => {
+		const declared = new Set(collectTokenNames(TOKEN_NAMES))
+		expect(CALIBRATED_TIERS.length).toBeGreaterThan(0)
+		expect(
+			CALIBRATED_TIERS.filter(([role, , tier]) => !declared.has(`--vn-color-${role}-${tier}`)),
+		).toEqual([])
+		expect(CALIBRATED_TIERS.filter(([, mode]) => mode !== 'light' && mode !== 'dark')).toEqual([])
+		expect(CALIBRATED_TIERS.filter(([, , , color]) => !/^okl(ch|ab)\(/u.test(color))).toEqual([])
+		expect(
+			new Set(CALIBRATED_TIERS.map(([role, mode, tier]) => `${role}-${mode}-${tier}`)).size,
+		).toBe(CALIBRATED_TIERS.length)
+	})
+	it('reads back the retained root and dark name lists from the installed Bootstrap stylesheet', () => {
+		const cascade = readBootstrapCascade()
+		expect(extractBootstrapVariables(cascade, 'root').map(([name]) => name)).toStrictEqual([
+			...BOOTSTRAP_ROOT_VARIABLES,
+		])
+		expect(extractBootstrapVariables(cascade, 'dark').map(([name]) => name)).toStrictEqual([
+			...BOOTSTRAP_DARK_VARIABLES,
+		])
+	})
+	it('carries one unique prefixed name per compatibility row, in each scope', () => {
+		expect(new Set(BOOTSTRAP_ROOT_VARIABLES).size).toBe(BOOTSTRAP_ROOT_VARIABLES.length)
+		expect(new Set(BOOTSTRAP_DARK_VARIABLES).size).toBe(BOOTSTRAP_DARK_VARIABLES.length)
+		expect(
+			[...BOOTSTRAP_ROOT_VARIABLES, ...BOOTSTRAP_DARK_VARIABLES].filter(
+				(name) => !name.startsWith('--bs-'),
+			),
+		).toEqual([])
+	})
+	it('names the Veneer dark additions outside the Bootstrap dark scope and inside its root scope', () => {
+		expect(THEME_DARK_ADDITIONS.filter((name) => BOOTSTRAP_DARK_VARIABLES.includes(name))).toEqual(
+			[],
+		)
+		expect(THEME_DARK_ADDITIONS.filter((name) => !BOOTSTRAP_ROOT_VARIABLES.includes(name))).toEqual(
+			[],
+		)
+	})
+	it('records for every retained row the value Bootstrap declares for its alias at root', () => {
+		const cascade = readBootstrapCascade()
+		const root = new Map(extractBootstrapVariables(cascade, 'root'))
+		const rows = [...RETAINED_COLOR_ALIASES, ...RETAINED_LENGTH_ALIASES]
+		expect(rows.length).toBeGreaterThan(0)
+		expect(rows.filter(([token]) => !token.startsWith('--vn-'))).toEqual([])
+		expect(
+			rows.flatMap(([token, alias, value]) =>
+				root.get(alias) === value ? [] : [[token, alias, value, root.get(alias)]],
+			),
+		).toEqual([])
+		expect(
+			RETAINED_COLOR_ALIASES.filter(([, alias]) =>
+				RETAINED_LENGTH_ALIASES.some(([, other]) => other === alias),
+			),
+		).toEqual([])
+	})
+	it('overrides in the customization recipe only names the token registry declares', () => {
+		const declared = new Set(collectTokenNames(TOKEN_NAMES))
+		const overridden: string[] = []
+		parse(CUSTOMIZATION_RECIPE).walkDecls((declaration) => {
+			if (declaration.prop.startsWith('--vn-')) overridden.push(declaration.prop)
+		})
+		expect(overridden.length).toBeGreaterThan(0)
+		expect(overridden.filter((name) => !declared.has(name))).toEqual([])
+		expect(CUSTOMIZATION_RECIPE).toContain('rgba(var(--vn-color-primary-rgb), 0.5)')
+		expect(FILL_ONLY_RECIPE).toContain('--vn-color-primary-base')
+		expect(FILL_ONLY_RECIPE).not.toContain('--vn-color-primary-rgb')
+	})
+	it('carries the customization fence the guide publishes, byte for byte', () => {
+		expect(readFileSync(VENEER_GUIDE_PATH, 'utf8')).toContain(CUSTOMIZATION_RECIPE)
+	})
+})

+// Calibration setup: the value lists and settled readings the compatibility oracle measures the
+// shipped cascade against. Host-independent: the Node `setup` project and the browser `src:styles`
+// project both load it, so it takes no `node:*` import and reads no document. Every value here was
+// copied from a source outside this workspace — Bootstrap's own stylesheet, Elements' built
+// showcase, or this package's guide — so a proof comparing a reading against one of these lists
+// compares two mechanisms rather than one mechanism against itself.
+
+/** Names the Bootstrap scopes the compatibility oracle reads. */
+export type BootstrapScope = 'root' | 'dark'
+
+/**
+ * Matches the selector each Bootstrap scope is written with, after the selector's whitespace is
+ * collapsed.
+ *
+ * @remarks
+ * The root pattern admits `:root` alone and `:root` at the head of a selector list, which is how
+ * Bootstrap writes `:root, [data-bs-theme=light]`. The dark pattern is anchored at the start, so a
+ * component rule painted inside a dark island — `.navbar-dark, .navbar[data-bs-theme=dark]` —
+ * is outside the scope while `[data-bs-theme=dark] .btn-close` is inside it.
+ */
+export const BOOTSTRAP_SCOPE_PATTERNS: Readonly<Record<BootstrapScope, RegExp>> = Object.freeze({
+	root: /^:root(,|$| )/u,
+	dark: /^\[data-bs-theme=dark\]/u,
+})
+
+/**
+ * Locates this package's guide, whose customization fence {@link CUSTOMIZATION_RECIPE} copies.
+ *
+ * @remarks
+ * A browser project loads this module, so it takes no `node:fs` import and the Node proof reads
+ * the file itself. `tests/setupConformance.ts` holds the release pin and the cascade digest for
+ * the same reason in reverse: it is Node-only, so a constant it declares cannot reach the browser
+ * project this module runs in.
+ */
+export const VENEER_GUIDE_PATH = 'guides/veneer.md'
+
+/**
+ * Names every `--bs-*` variable Bootstrap declares at `:root`, in declaration order.
+ *
+ * @remarks
+ * This is the union of every rule in Bootstrap 5.3.8's `dist/css/bootstrap.css` file whose
+ * selector begins `:root`, deduplicated at each name's first position, which is what the
+ * `extractBootstrapVariables` reader in `tests/setupStyles.ts` reads back and
+ * `tests/setupCalibration.test.ts` asserts this equals.
+ * Bootstrap repeats those names on `[data-bs-theme=light]` apart from the `--bs-breakpoint-*`
+ * rung, which it declares at `:root` alone, so the union covers `:root` and
+ * `[data-bs-theme=light]`, and the light theme adds nothing to it.
+ *
+ * The list is the compatibility contract the shipped cascade owes: the `--bs-` partition of
+ * Veneer's own `:root` equals it exactly.
+ */

+/**

+/**
+ * Records each color-valued token Veneer retains from Bootstrap, the alias it answers, and the
+ * value Bootstrap declares for that alias.
+ *
+ * @remarks
+ * The value is Bootstrap's own, and `tests/setupCalibration.test.ts` asserts each row against what
+ * the `extractBootstrapVariables` reader in `tests/setupStyles.ts` reads at `root` scope, so a
+ * Bootstrap bump reddens there. The
+ * browser proof compares the shipped cascade's resolved token against the same value, so a drifted
+ * retained value reddens there. The table is what carries the reading between the Node `setup`
+ * project and the browser `src:styles` project, because a Node project resolves a raw CSS import to
+ * an empty string.
+ *
+ * A row is a value claim rather than a naming claim: `--vn-form-valid` answers
+ * `--bs-form-valid-color` and takes Elements' success fill, so the pairing is Bootstrap's and the
+ * value is not, and that row is outside this table. `--bs-gray` and `--bs-gray-dark` carry no
+ * canonical token of their own, so each is paired with the ramp rung Veneer points it at.
+ *
+ * A retained alias whose value is text carries no row in either table. `--bs-font-monospace` and
+ * `--bs-gradient`, which `--vn-font-mono-base` and `--vn-surface-gradient` answer, resolve to a font
+ * stack and to a gradient image, and neither the painted-channel reading this table drives nor the
+ * pixel reading {@link RETAINED_LENGTH_ALIASES} drives reaches one. Veneer also writes the gradient
+ * over its own palette token, so that pair agrees as rendered rather than as text.
+ */

+import { readdirSync } from 'node:fs'
+import { requireValue } from '@orkestrel/test'
+import { TOKEN_NAMES } from '@src/core'
+import { parse } from 'postcss'
+import { compileString } from 'sass'
+import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
+import oracle from './fixtures/oracle/inventory.json'
+import { readBootstrapCascade, readBuiltCascade, readOracleInventory } from './setupConformance.js'
+import * as setup from './setupCases.js'
+import {
+	BREAKPOINT_CASES,
+	CONTAINER_CLASS_CASES,
+	CONTAINER_WIDTH_CASES,
+	ELEMENT_TAGS,
+	GAP_STEP_CASES,
+	GRID_BREAKPOINT_CASES,
+	GRID_COLUMN_STEPS,
+	GRID_COUNT_STEPS,
+	GRID_OFFSET_STEPS,
+	ICON_LINK_MARKUP,
+	IMAGE_PAINT_CASES,
+	LINK_OFFSET_CASES,
+	LINK_OPACITY_CASES,
+	LINK_ROLES,
+	LINK_UNDERLINE_OPACITY_CASES,
+	LIST_CLASS_CASES,
+	MANDATED_TAG_PAIRS,
+	QUOTE_FOOTER_CASES,
+	RATIO_CASES,
+	TABLE_GEOMETRY_CASES,
+	TABLE_MARKUP,
+	TABLE_RESPONSIVE_CASES,
+	TABLE_ROLES,
+	TABLE_ROLE_CASES,
+	TABLE_SELECTORS,
+	TYPE_DISPLAY_CASES,
+	TYPE_DISPLAY_TOKEN_CASES,
+	TYPE_HEADING_CASES,
+	TYPE_HEADING_TOKEN_CASES,
+	TYPE_MARK_CASES,
+	TYPE_MARK_TOKEN_CASES,
+	TYPE_TEXT_CASES,
+} from './setupCases.js'
+import {
+	compileBreakpointRamp,
+	extractBootstrapVariables,
+	normalizeComplexSelector,
+	parseMediaWidth,
+} from './setupStyles.js'
+
+describe('case setup', () => {
+	it('binds table case families and maximum widths to the inventory and ramp', async () => {
+		const ramp = await compileBreakpointRamp()
+		expect(
+			new Set([
+				...TABLE_SELECTORS,
+				...TABLE_ROLES.map((role) => `.table-${role}`),
+				...TABLE_RESPONSIVE_CASES.map(({ name }) => `.${name}`),
+			]),
+		).toEqual(new Set(oracle.components.table.selectors.map(({ selector }) => selector)))
+		for (const { name, boundary, readings } of TABLE_RESPONSIVE_CASES.filter(
+			(entry) => entry.boundary !== undefined,
+		)) {
+			if (boundary === undefined) throw new Error('A conditional wrapper has no boundary')
+			const recorded = oracle.components.table.selectors.find(
+				({ selector }) => selector === `.${name}`,
+			)
+			expect(recorded).toBeDefined()
+			expect(boundary).toBe(ramp.members.get(name.replace('table-responsive-', '')))
+			expect(
+				recorded && 'condition' in recorded ? parseMediaWidth(recorded.condition) : undefined,
+			).toBe(boundary - 0.02)
+			expect(readings).toEqual([boundary - 1, boundary, boundary + 1])
+		}
+		for (const { name, padding } of TABLE_GEOMETRY_CASES.filter(
+			(entry) => entry.name === 'table' || entry.name === 'table-sm',
+		)) {
+			const declarations = oracle.components.table.selectors.find(
+				({ selector }) => selector === `.${name} > :not(caption) > * > *`,
+			)?.declarations
+			expect(declarations?.find(({ property }) => property === 'padding')?.value).toBe(
+				`${String(padding / 16)}rem ${String(padding / 16)}rem`,
+			)
+		}
+		expect(TABLE_GEOMETRY_CASES.map(({ name }) => name)).toEqual([
+			'table',
+			'table-sm',
+			'table-bordered',
+			'table-borderless',
+		])
+		expect(TABLE_ROLE_CASES.map(({ role }) => role)).toEqual(TABLE_ROLES)
+		const palette = new Map(extractBootstrapVariables(readBootstrapCascade(), 'root'))
+		for (const entry of TABLE_ROLE_CASES) {
+			expect(entry.channels).toBe(palette.get(`--bs-${entry.role}-rgb`))
+			const declarations = oracle.components.table.selectors.find(
+				({ selector }) => selector === `.table-${entry.role}`,
+			)?.declarations
+			for (const [property, value] of [

+				['bg', entry.background],

+				['hover-bg', entry.hover],
+			])
+				expect(
+					declarations?.find(
+						(declaration) => declaration.property === `--bs-table-${String(property)}`,
+					)?.value,
+				).toBe(value)
+		}
+		for (const cases of [

+			TABLE_ROLES,
+			TABLE_ROLE_CASES,
+			TABLE_RESPONSIVE_CASES,

+			expect(Object.isFrozen(cases)).toBe(true)
+		}

+		}

+	it('binds every grid breakpoint and numbered family to the ramp and inventory', async () => {

+			GRID_BREAKPOINT_CASES.filter(({ boundary }) => boundary === 0).map(({ name }) => name),

+	it('binds every named ratio to the inventory, to its own name, and to the scale the family carries', () => {
+		const recorded = oracle.components.ratio.properties['--bs-aspect-ratio']
+		expect(RATIO_CASES.map(({ name }) => `.ratio-${name}`)).toEqual(
+			recorded.map(({ selector }) => selector),
+		)
+		expect(RATIO_CASES.map(({ aspect }) => aspect)).toEqual(recorded.map(({ value }) => value))
+		// The percentage is derived here rather than read from the record a second time, so a case
+		// whose name and percentage disagree reddens even where the record agrees with the case.
+		expect(
+			RATIO_CASES.map(
+				({ width, height }) => `${String(Math.round((height / width) * 100 * 1e10) / 1e10)}%`,
+			),
+		).toEqual(RATIO_CASES.map(({ aspect }) => aspect))
+		// The control on the scale: `_grid.scss` rounds its column percentages to eight decimal
+		// places, and this family's widest ratio is the value that separates the two scales.
+		expect(
+			RATIO_CASES.map(
+				({ width, height }) => `${String(Math.round((height / width) * 100 * 1e8) / 1e8)}%`,
+			),
+		).not.toEqual(RATIO_CASES.map(({ aspect }) => aspect))

+			(rule) => {

+					authored.set(rule.selector, value)
+				})
+			},
+		)
+		expect([...authored]).toEqual(RATIO_CASES.map(({ name, aspect }) => [`.ratio-${name}`, aspect]))
+		// The build shortens every percentage to six significant digits, which the grid's shipped
+		// column widths carry too, so the shipped text is read as the number it names.
+		const shipped = new Map<string, string>()
+		parse(readBuiltCascade()).walkRules((rule) => {
+			rule.walkDecls('--bs-aspect-ratio', ({ value }) => {
+				shipped.set(rule.selector, value)
+			})
+		})
+		for (const { name, aspect } of RATIO_CASES) {
+			const value = requireValue(shipped.get(`.ratio-${name}`), `No shipped .ratio-${name}`)
+			expect(Number.parseFloat(value)).toBeCloseTo(Number.parseFloat(aspect), 3)
+		}
+		expect(Object.isFrozen(RATIO_CASES)).toBe(true)
+		for (const entry of RATIO_CASES) expect(Object.isFrozen(entry)).toBe(true)

+	it('carries the official icon-link classes and the undefined icon class in the mounted markup', () => {

+		// Every class the markup carries beyond the icon's own belongs to this key's recorded

+			oracle.components['icon-link'].selectors.flatMap(({ classes: names }) => names),

+			oracle.components['icon-link'].selectors.filter(({ selector }) => selector.includes('.bi')),
+		).not.toEqual([])
+		// The combinator ships and the class it names stays undeclared, so no rule selects the icon
+		// class on its own. Comparing whole selectors rather than the text is what separates the
+		// undeclared class from the combinators that mention it: every selector carrying `.bi` names
+		// a Veneer class beside it.
+		const selectors = new Set<string>()
+		parse(readBuiltCascade()).walkRules((rule) => {
+			for (const selector of rule.selectors) selectors.add(normalizeComplexSelector(selector))

+				'.icon-link > .bi',
+				'.icon-link-hover:hover > .bi',
+				'.icon-link-hover:focus-visible > .bi',
+			].sort(),
+		)
+	})

+	it('freezes each component case table and gives it the rows its own family ships', () => {

+			'display-4',
+			'display-5',
+			'display-6',
+		])
+		// Each retune row names the token its own level reads, so the table is the matrix the type
+		// proof walks and a level missing from it is a level nothing retunes.
+		expect(TYPE_HEADING_TOKEN_CASES.map((entry) => [entry.twin, entry.token])).toEqual([
+			['h1', '--vn-size-8'],
+			['h2', '--vn-size-7'],

+			['display-5', '--vn-display-5'],
+			['display-6', '--vn-display-6'],
+		])
+		// Each mark retune row names the painting property it moves and the token that property
+		// reads, so a row missing from the table is a mark token nothing retunes.
+		expect(TYPE_MARK_TOKEN_CASES.map((entry) => [entry.property, entry.token])).toEqual([
+			['color', '--vn-text-mark'],
+			['background-color', '--vn-surface-mark'],
+		])

+		expect(LIST_CLASS_CASES.map((entry) => entry.name)).toEqual(['list-unstyled', 'list-inline'])
+		for (const table of [TYPE_MARK_CASES, QUOTE_FOOTER_CASES, IMAGE_PAINT_CASES]) {
+			expect(table.map((entry) => entry.mode)).toEqual(['light', 'dark'])
+		}
+		// The footer keeps one gray in both modes, so the table declares that rather than leaving a
+		// reader to infer it from two rows that happen to agree.
+		expect(new Set(QUOTE_FOOTER_CASES.map((entry) => entry.color)).size).toBe(1)
+		// A retune row discriminates only by naming a size no level resolves by default: a row set
+		// to a default reads the same whether its level names its own token, a sibling's token, or a
+		// literal, so the retune matrix would pass while proving none of them. The disjointness is
+		// what arms that matrix, and nothing else asserts it.
+		// The set is widened to `string` on purpose: the frozen tables carry literal types, so a
+		// narrowly typed set refuses the reading outright and the invariant would rest on the two
+		// unions staying disjoint rather than on an assertion a changed row can fail.
+		const defaults = new Set<string>([
+			...TYPE_HEADING_CASES.map((entry) => entry.size),
+			...TYPE_DISPLAY_CASES.map((entry) => entry.size),
+		])
+		expect(

+				.filter((entry) => defaults.has(entry.value))
+				.map((entry) => entry.value),
+		).toEqual([])
+		// The mark retune rows carry the same invariant over paint: a row set to the system color
+		// its token already resolves to reads the same whether the treatment names the token or
+		// carries the keyword, so the retune would pass while proving neither.
+		const painted = new Set<string>(TYPE_MARK_CASES.flatMap((entry) => Object.values(entry.values)))
+		expect(
+			TYPE_MARK_TOKEN_CASES.filter((entry) => painted.has(entry.value)).map((entry) => entry.value),
+		).toEqual([])
+		for (const table of [
+			TYPE_HEADING_CASES,
+			TYPE_HEADING_TOKEN_CASES,
+			TYPE_DISPLAY_CASES,
+			TYPE_DISPLAY_TOKEN_CASES,
+			TYPE_TEXT_CASES,
+			TYPE_MARK_CASES,
+			TYPE_MARK_TOKEN_CASES,
+			LIST_CLASS_CASES,
+			QUOTE_FOOTER_CASES,
+			IMAGE_PAINT_CASES,
+		]) {
+			expect(Object.isFrozen(table)).toBe(true)
+			expect(table.every((entry) => Object.isFrozen(entry))).toBe(true)
+		}
+	})

+	it('names every partial the elements layer ships, and gives each mandated child a legal parent', () => {
+		const partials = readdirSync('src/styles/elements')
+			.flatMap((name) =>
+				name.startsWith('_') && name.endsWith('.scss') ? [name.slice(1, -5)] : [],
+			)
+			.sort()
+		expect(partials.length).toBeGreaterThan(0)
+		expect(ELEMENT_TAGS.map(([partial]) => partial)).toEqual(partials)
+		expect(ELEMENT_TAGS.filter(([, tags]) => tags.length === 0)).toEqual([])
+		expect(
+			ELEMENT_TAGS.flatMap(([, tags]) => tags).filter((tag) => !/^[a-z][a-z1-6]*$/u.test(tag)),
+		).toEqual([])
+		// A row carrying a tag the content model gives only one parent carries that parent too, so
+		// the rendered position proof mounts the child where the markup can actually write it.
+		for (const [partial, tags] of ELEMENT_TAGS) {
+			for (const tag of tags) {
+				const parents = MANDATED_TAG_PAIRS.filter(([, descendant]) => descendant === tag)
+				if (parents.length === 0) continue
+				expect(
+					parents.some(([ancestor]) => tags.includes(ancestor)),
+					`${partial} names ${tag} without a legal parent`,
+				).toBe(true)
+			}
+		}
+	})
+	it('freezes each mandated pair as a distinct ancestor and descendant written once', () => {
+		expect(MANDATED_TAG_PAIRS.length).toBeGreaterThan(0)
+		expect(Object.isFrozen(MANDATED_TAG_PAIRS)).toBe(true)
+		expect(MANDATED_TAG_PAIRS.every((pair) => Object.isFrozen(pair))).toBe(true)
+		expect(MANDATED_TAG_PAIRS.filter(([ancestor, descendant]) => ancestor === descendant)).toEqual(
+			[],
+		)
+		expect(new Set(MANDATED_TAG_PAIRS.map((pair) => pair.join(' '))).size).toBe(
+			MANDATED_TAG_PAIRS.length,
+		)
+		// A mandated pair is a containment claim, so the reverse of one is never mandated too: the
+		// table reading both ways would excuse every ordering it names.
+		expect(
+			MANDATED_TAG_PAIRS.filter(([ancestor, descendant]) =>
+				MANDATED_TAG_PAIRS.some(([outer, inner]) => outer === descendant && inner === ancestor),
+			),
+		).toEqual([])
+	})
+})
diff --git a/tests/setupCases.ts b/tests/setupCases.ts

+// Case setup: the frozen case tables and markup strings the style proofs drive their scenarios
+// from. Host-independent: the Node `setup` project and the browser `src:styles` project both load
+// it, so it takes no `node:*` import and reads no document. A reader that walks a cascade belongs
+// in `tests/setupStyles.ts`; a value list the oracle calibrates belongs in
+// `tests/setupCalibration.ts`.

+/**
+ * Pins each named aspect ratio against the percentage its own name derives.
+ *
+ * @remarks
+ * The `aspect` field is the authored percentage, which is the height over the width at the ten
+ * decimal places Sass emits. The build's own numeric precision then shortens the shipped value to
+ * six significant digits, so a case reading the cascade compares the ratio the resolved box holds
+ * rather than this text.
+ */

+/**
+ * Holds the icon-link markup the proofs mount, with the icon child the published combinator selects.
+ *
+ * @remarks
+ * The `bi` class is Bootstrap Icons' own and Veneer declares no rule for it, so the markup carries
+ * the class a consumer brings rather than one this package defines. The bare anchor and the bare
+ * icon beside the pair are the controls that tell the class treatment apart from what the `a` and
+ * `svg` element rules already give every anchor and every icon.

+/**
+ * Holds the module specifiers the walk in flight has read, in source order.
+ *
+ * @remarks
+ * A `Visitor` handler is given the node and nothing else, so the walk and the reading it produces
+ * cannot be joined by an argument. {@link extractSpecifiers} empties this before every walk and
+ * takes the readings out of it with `splice`, so the array is empty between calls and the list a
+ * caller receives is its own. A case asserting on it directly reads an empty array, which is the
+ * state the reader leaves it in.
+ */
+export const SPECIFIER_READINGS: string[] = []
+
+/**
+ * Reads every literal import, re-export, dynamic import, and `require` specifier into
+ * {@link SPECIFIER_READINGS}.
+ */
+export const SPECIFIER_VISITOR = new Visitor({
+	ImportDeclaration(node) {
+		SPECIFIER_READINGS.push(node.source.value)
+	},
+	ExportNamedDeclaration(node) {
+		if (node.source !== null) SPECIFIER_READINGS.push(node.source.value)
+	},
+	ExportAllDeclaration(node) {
+		SPECIFIER_READINGS.push(node.source.value)
+	},
+	ImportExpression(node) {
+		const specifier = extractStringArgument(node.source)
+		if (specifier !== undefined) SPECIFIER_READINGS.push(specifier)
+	},
+	TSImportType(node) {
+		SPECIFIER_READINGS.push(node.source.value)
+	},
+	TSExternalModuleReference(node) {
+		SPECIFIER_READINGS.push(node.expression.value)
+	},
+	CallExpression(node) {
+		if (node.callee.type !== 'Identifier' || node.callee.name !== 'require') return
+		const argument = node.arguments[0]
+		if (argument === undefined) return
+		const specifier = extractStringArgument(argument)
+		if (specifier !== undefined) SPECIFIER_READINGS.push(specifier)
+	},
+})
+

 /**
@@ -470,6 +507,34 @@ export function scanForbiddenSource(source: string, names: readonly string[]): s
 	)

+/**
+ * Scans a built JavaScript entry for a forbidden runtime, by import specifier or by signature.
+ * @param path - The built entry to read, such as `dist/src/browser/index.js`.
+ * @param names - Exact package names or scope prefixes ending in a slash.
+ * @param signatures - Identifiers a forbidden runtime leaves in bundled output, such as
+ * {@link FORBIDDEN_SIGNATURES}.
+ * @returns The forbidden specifier {@link scanForbiddenSource} reports, or failing that the first
+ * signature the entry's text carries, or `undefined` when the entry carries neither.
+ * @throws The filesystem `ENOENT` when `path` does not exist, and the parse error
+ * {@link extractSpecifiers} raises when the entry is not a module.
+ * @remarks
+ * `npm run build:src` precedes any call reading a `dist/` entry, the way the presence cases already
+ * assume their built cascade. A specifier scan alone cannot judge a built entry, because a bundler
+ * inlines the package it resolves and leaves no specifier to find; the signature pass is what reads
+ * the code that arrived in its place. A specifier still survives for a runtime left external, which
+ * is why both passes run.
+ */
+export function scanForbiddenBuild(
+	path: string,
+	names: readonly string[],
+	signatures: readonly string[],
+): string | undefined {
+	const built = readFileSync(path, 'utf8')
+	return (
+		scanForbiddenSource(built, names) ?? signatures.find((signature) => built.includes(signature))
+	)
+}

-/**
